186 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; JOHANNES KEPLER

to have learned letters <note>Continuing the thought from the previous page regarding the renewal of culture.</note>. The primary languages, Greek and Latin, were publicly decaying, solely due to the scarcity of books and the inundation of barbarians. How many of the most famous works of the ancients, how many histories, how many books of the theologians—indeed, how many entire libraries—were lost through this sloth, so that today no hope remains of recovering them? 

But look now at the wonderful transformation of things from these last 150 years <note>Kepler, writing in 1606, is looking back to the mid-15th century (c. 1450s), the era of the fall of Constantinople and the invention of the printing press.</note>. First, the laws of Empires were improved, especially the German ones; a holy and stable public peace was established, robberies were forbidden, courts were organized, and most healthy customs were fixed—among which the institution of couriers or postal carriers <term>veredarii</term> <note>original: "veredariorum". Kepler refers to the establishment of the Imperial Post, which revolutionized communication across Europe.</note> is not the least. 

Even the Turkish nation, having stripped off its barbarism, has learned civility. Europe began to weigh and recognize its own strengths, and to use counsel more than force, especially after the loss of Constantinople and the overturning of the Greek Empire. Machines of war <note>Meaning artillery and firearms.</note> were invented, and their use was refined by many hands to the highest degree of efficiency. The Turkish nation made the greatest progress through vigilance, industry, and courage; while the Greeks were either weakened or distrustful among themselves, just as the Europeans were. 

The Spanish expelled the Moors; with the greatest mental exertion they devoted themselves to nautical matters. They first discovered the route to the East Indies through the work of many years—first by sending explorers through Africa, then by equipping ships and circumnavigating Africa. Fortune favored such great industry, with the West Indies <note>The Americas.</note> being discovered; from these things European commerce has reached the highest peak through incredible growth; certainly, the minds of all were set on fire for these reasons which I now attribute to the heavens.

The art of printing alone easily proves that the industry of men at that time was ineffable, if anyone considers a little more diligently how many hands the art requires; indeed, how many copies are perfected by so few hands; finally, how many steps there are from those seals—by which Scaliger <note>Joseph Justus Scaliger (1540–1609), a famous scholar who speculated that printing evolved from ancient signet rings or stamps.</note> believes our ancestors were first prompted toward typography—to the modern magnificence of the art. 

With printing born, books became common; hence, everyone everywhere throughout Europe applied themselves to the study of letters; hence, so many Academies were born; so many learned men suddenly stood forth, that in a short time the authority of those who retained barbarism fell away. Nor did the desires of men rest until the author of a new order <note>Kepler likely refers here to the Protestant Reformation and the subsequent educational reforms.</note> appeared, who worked professionally in the study of letters; from which time almost all the authority of the religious orders passed to this new order—except insofar as those older orders, busy with their own affairs, also applied themselves to the study of letters. 

From navigations and commerce, the occasion was offered to Europeans to propagate the Christian faith most widely among barbarous and hitherto unknown nations. On the other hand, from the Academies, the freedom of debating, the abundance of books, and the convenience of printing—assuredly from erudition and public restlessness—there finally arose that great [movement] <note>Sentence continues on the next page.</note> and in all ages...

<summary>Kepler reflects on the cultural and scientific explosion of the Renaissance (roughly 1450–1600), citing printing, navigation, and legal reforms as evidence of a "wonderful transformation" mirrored in the heavens.</summary>

**ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT-BEARER. 187**

...the memorable separation of many European provinces from the Roman see <note>Kepler is referring to the Protestant Reformation, which began roughly a century before this book was published.</note>. I am fully persuaded that the matter itself, along with the circumstances described, proceeded from on high—that is, from divine providence—and is not to be confined within the narrow limits of natural causes. Nor do those who condemn it deny this. Yet there is something universal in the matter itself, visible through all other human affairs: namely, the restlessness and fervor of men, which is especially prominent during these times. I shall speak of this alone for now, as I recount the examples by which that fervor is proven. For when the minds of all were stirred by celestial stimuli, and neighbors were easily joined together for whatever cause, there arose so many seditions, factions, disagreements, and wars, both for the sake of religion and of empires.

Among the Muslims themselves—who today, along with Christians and Jews, divide the multitude of the human race into roughly three equal parts—has there not been kindled in this century a new torch of civil wars, arising from differences in their religion, which seems destined to bring ruin to the Turks? And because in the years 1504, 1524, and 1563 there were <term>significant conjunctions</term> <note>original: "capitales conjunctiones". These are alignments of the "superior" planets (Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn) which astronomers believed triggered major historical shifts.</note>, I believe it was all the easier for a common cause to arise from them, which unites the love and the minds of many nations separated by vast distances. Thus, whenever battles are fought in France, the Netherlands, or the British Isles, there is no lack of people in Hungary, Poland, and Germany who, with opposing prayers, follow the fortune of their respective sides and believe their own interests are at stake—even though, if they lacked a common enemy, they would be fighting among themselves.

<margin>
To what extent the modern restlessness of the human race is attributed to the great conjunctions of our time.
</margin>

You will say these are the works of the Holy Spirit, who gathers nations of all different languages into the unity of faith. I do not deny this, and I would attribute everything to this Spirit, were there not so many such things that are of evil; if this same zeal were not found in both parties, who treat each other with hostile infection, which is not from the Holy Spirit. Or is it a small thing that even the antiquities—which today are either defended most steadfastly or unearthed most stubbornly—now shine brightly? All the soot gathered over the long passage of time has been scrubbed away and they have been dressed in brilliant colors, so that if you trust your eyes alone, you would pronounce everything to be new. Has it ever been heard of that opposing parties, not very far apart in their own regions, would go with equipped fleets to the <term>Antipodes</term> <note>The opposite side of the world; Kepler is referring to the global scale of colonial and religious wars in the Americas and the Indies.</note> to fight over the highest matters of state, as if it were not just as easy to die at home? Did a <term>Sarmatian</term> <note>Referring to the peoples of Eastern Europe/Poland.</note> or German fleet ever set sail from the Baltic Sea during the height of the Roman Empire, circumnavigate Europe, and arrive in Italy, outstripping the runners who went by the shortest inland routes through the Mediterranean lands, and relieve the high cost of grain by bringing in supplies? What does the antiquity of the past have that is similar to today's science of military affairs? What shall I say of today's mechanical arts, innumerable in their quantity and incomprehensible in their subtlety? Are we not today rescuing all the ancient writers, as many as still exist, and bringing them to light through the art of printing?

<margin>No unforeseen change can be predicted solely from the fiery trigon.</margin>

Therefore, having weighed these matters, I have pronounced astrologers to be foolish if they think we should expect more or greater things in the coming 200 years than occurred in the past 150, by the natural power of the <term>Fiery Trigon</term> <note>original: "Trigoni ignei". <margin>Examples from the age of Christ.</margin>

Through a comparison of the times of Christ and of Charlemagne, which Fabricius <note>David Fabricius (1564–1617), a German astronomer and correspondent of Kepler.</note> uses, the matter will become clear. In the 40th Julian year, five years before the Christian era, there was a great conjunction in Pisces; this could have been taken for the start of a Fiery Trigon just as the one in 1583 could. Yet the Empire of the Romans had already passed from the people to the family of the Caesars more than 40 years earlier. Nor did anything new happen then; for the empire remained in this family for another 70 years; it remained with Augustus for another 19 years; and with other Roman families of Princes for a very long time.

<margin>Of Charlemagne.</margin>

So too, when the fiery triplicity <note>Another term for the "Fiery Trigon" or group of three fire signs.</note> returned around the year of Christ 809: nothing new occurred, except that Charlemagne—who had already transferred the Western Empire to his father Pepin in 751, and the Roman Empire to himself in 801—stabilized it with distinguished successes. 

Rather, putting aside the names of constellations and Trigons, whether guided by fire or water, I have bid men to look at the things themselves as they are currently prepared. For some things are striving toward their peak; some are verging toward ruin; and of some, a long duration can be hoped for. Therefore, that stimulus of the conjunction in Sagittarius will chiefly disturb and unsettle men in those matters they already have at hand. But as for those things which might arise entirely anew by the occasion of this celestial provocation: they cannot be greater (as long as the efficacy of the Fiery Trigon alone dominates) than those which happened shortly before.

**ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT-BEARER. 189**

...to explain more broadly this pronouncement concerning the natural effect of the <term>Fiery Trigon</term> <note>original: "Trigoni ignei." A 200-year astrological cycle where the conjunctions of Jupiter and Saturn occur in the "fire" signs: Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius.</note> (and thus also of the star). The more those who defend antiquity grow strong, the more closely those who defend liberty will unite; and the more prosperous the fortune these groups enjoy, the more bitterly they will bite and consume one another in turn. It may then happen that new factions and new opinions emerge. Although the zeal for contention among politicians is, as said above, in decline, there will arise "composers" <note>Kepler likely refers to mediators or those attempting to synthesize differing views, though he warns they may inadvertently cause more trouble.</note> who will themselves stir up no fewer disturbances than if they were advocating a new sect.

Not insignificant changes will occur; as some magistrates die out, others of a different profession <note>Here "profession" refers to religious or political affiliation.</note> will succeed them, dragging their provinces along with them and un-teaching what those people had previously learned.

I say all these things as one who looks at nature alone. Meanwhile, I am not unmindful of divine providence; for while it sometimes uses nature as a tool it has itself provided, it certainly does not receive its laws from Nature for dispensing its punishments and mercy among those who have pleased it.

Indeed, those knowledgeable in affairs think that the empire of the Turks <note>The Ottoman Empire.</note> <margin>Whether the Mahometan sect is soon to perish by the force of the Fiery Trigon.</margin> has lost much of its former strength. Therefore, in this matter, I accidentally agree with those who, from the stars, threaten destruction to that empire. 

Those astrologers indeed look back to the empty name of "fire." They think that because this dogma <note>Referring to Islam.</note> began under a Watery Trigon, it must perish under a Fiery one. Yet they do not consider that it already endured and grew strong once before under a Fiery Trigon, from 800 to 1000 AD. They say that the doctrine of Christ began under a fiery cycle, which lasted for the first 200 years, and that the same faith will be spread more widely under this current fiery cycle from 1600 to 1800. Again, they forget that throughout the last 100 years under the Watery Trigon, Christianity has been disseminated further across the globe through the skill of navigation—regarding the physical extent of the Earth—than it had since its very origin.

Even though I both hope and entirely believe that the power of the Ottoman family is now collapsing, I see no natural causes from the Fiery Trigon by which it can be proven that those who embrace the Mahometan doctrine <note>original: "Mahometicam doctrinam."</note> are more likely to fall under Christian rather than Persian rule. Whether, however, something better is to be expected now that the new star has shone forth, I defer to the following chapter. Prosperous rumors have already often been heard of a Persian Shah becoming a Christian; but...

...but those writings were produced with such negligence and such a disregard for plausibility that it easily appears they are the wishes of the simple-minded rather than the accounts of those with experience. <margin>Which regions the Fiery Trigon threatens.</margin>

Therefore, as far as it pertains to individual regions over which the Fiery Trigon is said to have significance: although there are some slender supports for assigning certain regions to certain celestial signs—at least as far as the horoscopes of rulers and the corresponding horoscopes of their subjects can provide—nevertheless, I think it is clear from what has been said (and as I emphasized three years ago) that we have no need to consult the "Catalogs of Cities and Regions" which are believed to be under the Fiery Trigon, and specifically under <term>Sagittarius</term> <note>The astrological sign where the Great Conjunction of 1603 occurred. Indeed, nothing new has occurred thus far in Stuttgart, or Meissen <note>A region in Saxony, Germany.</note>, or in Spain itself, even though they are referred to as being under Sagittarius.

On the contrary, let everyone look within themselves and examine their own fatherland to see if it has been observed for a long time that dissensions are smoldering, and fraud and hidden wars are swelling up. For whoever finds their name written in *that* catalog <note>Kepler means the "catalog" of existing political and social unrest, rather than the "catalog" of astrological signs.</note> has reason to fear this conjunction; not because the stars create the evil, but because the conjunction brings such provocations that evils, hitherto well-hidden, break out into the open.

Therefore, the fact that we have seen Hungary burning with seditions and Moravia with plundering was caused by the very things I have already mentioned, situated within the people themselves. Sagittarius did no harm here to Hungary, Košice, or Moravia. For those evils also reached Austria and Styria; the former of which is assigned to <term>Libra</term> and the latter to <term>Capricorn</term>; neither of which are under Sagittarius, the host of the conjunction.

And by this single event, I consider my cause—which I have here divided from the common cause of the Astrologers—to be wonderfully proven. I have, however, allowed for a distinction among men: those whom this place of conjunction touches in the primary locations of their horoscopes—provided they are also of an active and harmonic nature—may feel the primary provocations from this conjunction.

<meta>This page transitions from the physical/natural analysis of the New Star and the Fiery Trigon to a more speculative, theological inquiry. Kepler shifts his focus from how the star behaves as a natural object to why God might have "ignited" it as a providential sign for humanity.</meta>

**ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT-BEARER. 191**

at that time especially, the prophecies of the astrologers concerning the <term>Fiery Trigon</term> <note>The cycle of Jupiter-Saturn conjunctions in fire signs (Aries, Leo, Sagittarius).</note> were being widely circulated. Now, however, it will be further discussed what exactly is to be gathered from that same <term>Great Conjunction</term> <note>The meeting of Jupiter and Saturn, the "great" planets.</note>; especially after God, the Greatest and Best, made an immense distinction between this and previous conjunctions by placing a new star at the very location of the conjunction. Here, although I will indulge in conjectures a little more freely than I have until now, there will nevertheless be no reason for you to accuse me of contradicting myself. For until now, I have used principles that are natural, by the confession of the astrologers themselves; now, however, we shall adopt more than natural principles. Yet, in both cases, here and there, conjectures will be formed under the condition of a true supposition.

->*For what purpose the New Star was principally ignited.*<-

**CHAPTER XXX.**

<note>A decorative initial 'Q' is set within a square frame featuring a floral motif and a cherub's face, common in early 17th-century printing.</note> What we said in chapters 28 and 29 regarding the effects of the New <margin>The foundation of all divination through non-natural means or modes.</margin> Star treated this star as one of the natural causes; and those things are consistent with whichever of the opinions from chapter 26 may hold true. Now, however, we shall say certain things that will carry weight only with those who are persuaded that this star was ignited not by chance, nor by the necessity of nature, but by a certain plan and the special providence of God. Once this foundation is laid, a very wide field opens to us for the whole of the <term>Mantic art</term> <note>The art of prophecy or divination.</note>.

For most types of divination among the nations rested on this foundation: that through dreams, through the entrails of victims, through the meeting of animals, through monstrous births, through lightning, storms of wind, floods of water, earthquakes, celestial portents, Comets, and similar things, the Gods were believed to give answers to men. The word <term>to be divinely inspired</term> <note>original: "τὸ ἐπιθειάζειν" (to epitheiazein). A Greek term used by the historian Dio Cassius meaning to act or speak under divine influence or to interpret events as divine.</note> is very frequent in Dio, which signifies this very thing. Especially memorable is that prayer of Cyrus, which testifies either to the theology of the Persians or to that of Xenophon himself. For Cyrus, when he had received the announcement of his death and was performing a sacrifice, spoke thus: *O Father Jove <note>original: "Iupiter patrie." Jupiter of the ancestors or the fatherland.</note>, and you Sun, and all you Gods, receive these sacrificial victims, with which I, having performed many and great deeds, happily place the final touch, and at the same time I show myself grateful to you: because you have given signs of your will to me through the entrails of victims, through celestial portents, through auguries, and through oracles; what I ought to do, and what I ought to omit. Indeed, I have much gratitude to you, because I too have recognized your providence toward me. Therefore, to those same Gods who gave these responses, and through those things and animals by which signs*

<summary>Kepler moves from naturalistic astrology to a providential view, arguing that the New Star of 1604 was a deliberate sign from God. He justifies this "more than natural" inquiry by citing historical traditions of divination and divine signs, such as the prayer of Cyrus the Great from Xenophon.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Kepler’s transition from physical astronomy to the philosophical and theological implications of celestial signs. He is critiquing the foundations of astrology, distinguishing between natural laws and the "will" behind supernatural omens.</meta>

192 &emsp; &emsp; &emsp; &emsp; &emsp; &emsp; &emsp; **JOHANNES KEPLER**

...[they] used to publish, the power and direction were assigned to a higher cause. On the contrary, if it became clear that the movement or appearance of an animal or a portending object flowed from a natural cause—such that suspicion of supernatural governance was absent—people would settle into a quiet state, their minds released from anxiety. This was unless some natural connection was perhaps detected between the sign and the thing signified, which would not require the decision of a free and rational cause.

<margin>The foundation of superstitious divination from the stars.</margin>

&emsp; &emsp; A large part of astrology was born from the neglect of this foundation. For since many celestial portents had inspired faith that the Gods were speaking with the human race through these heavenly things, the unskilled began to attribute whatever happened in the heavens to the free will of the Gods and to interpret it as an omen. This was especially true if a sight struck the vision forcefully by its novelty or rarity, such as <term>eclipses</term> <note>Kepler refers to these as "eclipses of the lights," meaning the Sun and Moon.</note> or the gathering of many stars. If they had weighed from the beginning that all these things revolve by a fixed law of Nature, they would have either sought a natural connection between these celestial events and the outcomes that usually follow, or, if they could not find one, they would never have fallen into such credulity.

&emsp; &emsp; But others progressed into something worse, moving from folly into impiety. When they saw that the movements of the stars depend on natural necessity, yet still wished to count them among portents, they introduced a *god from the machine* <note>original: "θεὸς ἀπὸ μηχανῆς" (theos apo mechanes)—a theatrical device where a god is lowered onto the stage to resolve a plot. Kepler uses it here to mock the forced introduction of supernatural explanations for natural events.</note> once again. They did not suggest a god who freely shaped the movements of the heavens to signal things to us through them, but rather one who used the pattern of celestial movements as a blueprint for shaping and administering human affairs. From this come those many thousands of "astral spirits"—or more truly, legions of infernal devils—to whom that execrable Magic (falsely called "celestial") teaches one to offer specific sacrifices, pour out specific prayers, celebrate specific feasts, light candles during the days and hours of the planets, and wear clothes of the appropriate color. Surely it is only by this method—with demons cooperating, of course—that those most foolish and ridiculous decrees of the Astrologers concerning the <term>houses</term> <note>The twelve divisions of the sky in a horoscope.</note> and the dominations of the planets are verified. It is finally time to come to one's senses! The first inventors of these trifles were not so unskilled as I accused them above; rather, impiety provided them with a kind of understanding. But you, oh Christian Astrologers, are like children: having thrown away the kernel, you fixate on the shell. You embrace a corpse once the soul has been driven out; you practice this foolish and trifling astrology, which uses the houses of the planets, but you do so without the Magic <note>Kepler is mocking contemporary astrologers who use the "rules" of traditional astrology (like planetary houses) while rejecting the demonic "magic" that originally gave those rules their supposed power.</note>. Go ahead, "wash the fur coat, but try not to let it soak up the water" <note>A proverbial expression for trying to do something impossible or contradictory—here, trying to use superstitious systems while remaining a "rational" Christian.</note>.

<margin>God acts with men through signs.</margin>

&emsp; &emsp; I believe it is clear that there is a place for conjectures only when it is established that some intelligent Nature, through these signs upon which the mind of the interpreter is focused, wishes to speak with us—and that those signs are subject to that Nature's will and power. Thus, God showed to Pharaoh what He was about to do through a dream...

<summary>Kepler critiques the superstitious origins of astrology, arguing that celestial events are governed by natural laws rather than "astral spirits" or demons. He mocks "Christian astrologers" who follow hollow rules (like planetary houses) that were originally tied to occult practices they now claim to reject.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, natural law, superstition, omens, magic, astral spirits, Christian astrologers, planetary houses, divine signs</keywords>

...dream, Genesis 41:25. <note>original: "Gen. 41. v. 25." Kepler refers to Joseph interpreting Pharaoh's dream about the seven years of plenty and famine.</note> Thus He indicated to Nebuchadnezzar in a dream those things that were to come in the final times, Daniel 2:28. Thus He speaks with us even today through the bow of the Iris <note>The rainbow.</note>, and makes us certain that the human race shall not perish by a flood, Genesis 9. Thus He exhibits signs in the Sun, the Moon, and the stars, and moves the powers of the heavens, so that we might lift up our heads and see whether our redemption draws near. <note>A reference to the apocalyptic signs mentioned in Luke 21:25-28.</note>

&emsp; &emsp; To this same purpose belong portents of every kind, monsters, and dreams sent by divine will. Whatever of these is offered to men of its own accord, unsought—since indeed such things cannot be procured by us—I would judge, not absurdly, that they can be attributed either to God Himself, or to His ministering angels, or to some spirit which, connected to men and the whole world by a certain law of nature, acts by instinct rather than by reasoning. Therefore, the significations of those things are not to be rejected, unless it appears that they are accustomed to occur through the mere habit of nature.

<margin>
*What the cause*
*might be why*
*dreams and*
*portents*
*signify*
*through*
*allegories,*
*not clearly.*
</margin>

&emsp; &emsp; However, as to why I might doubt whether the spirit presiding over these signifying matters is "free," or whether it is naturally bound to the body of the world, I have weighty reasons on both sides. For those things which are signified, as much through dreams as through omens and monsters, are signified through <term>allegory</term> <note>Representing one thing under the image of another; a symbolic "riddle."</note>, not directly, clearly, or lucidly. If Nature, playing with allegories and pictures of things, is of her own will, and could explain the matter clearly if she wished, then the reason for this obscurity must be sought. Does she think it expedient that men be warned in a general sense, but not made certain of the specific details before the event has occurred? Yet she could have warned thus by a shorter shortcut, such as certain <term>genii</term> <note>In the classical sense: guiding spirits or minor divinities associated with a person or place.</note> are said to use, signaling an imminent downfall with a single pulse or beat. Why then these windings and riddles in dreams and monsters, conducted through those circumstances by which one usually arrives at a specific sight? Or is it true what Plutarch suggested to us, while discussing the lost cause of the Oracles, by bringing forward these verses from Sophocles:

&emsp; &emsp; *To the wise, fate speaks through riddles;*
&emsp; &emsp; *To the fool, a teacher is entirely useless.*
&emsp; &emsp; <note>original Greek: "Σοφοῖς μὲν αἰνικτῆρα θεσπίζειν τύχην / σκαιοῖς δὲ φαῦλον κἀν βραχεῖ διδάσκαλον." Kepler translates this into Latin to argue that divine signs require an active, reasoning mind to decode.</note>

&emsp; &emsp; That is to say: the wise man is hindered by no riddles of the Oracles, while for the fool, no explanation is sufficient for understanding. Doubtless, whatever "Nature" it is that speaks with us through the pictures of dreams and the allusions of portents, it is credible that she immensely favors the reasoning nature of humans because of its similarity to herself. Therefore, she signifies calamities through riddles so that men might be moved to reason about the causes (which is the proper work of a human being). Thus, once the true causes are known—especially if they reside within the man himself—they may think upon genuine remedies. For men, positioned in ambiguity by these labyrinths of significations, look around...

<summary>Kepler discusses why divine signs, dreams, and celestial portents are delivered in allegories and riddles rather than clear language. He concludes that this obscurity is intended to provoke human reason and the search for underlying causes.</summary>

...they observe the present; they gather the past, and place the future before their eyes, until they finally arrive at the thing itself.

&emsp; &emsp; Indeed, this is God’s most frequent custom—to whom it did not seem good to speak to men daily in explicit words, especially regarding natural things and those pertaining to the sustenance of this life. 

<margin>
What are God’s

He uses another kind of speech, through the course of nature and the motion of [celestial] bodies; and another kind of writing, which does not have twenty-three elements <note>The Latin alphabet of Kepler's time typically consisted of 23 letters (A B C D E F G H I K L M N O P Q R S T V X Y Z).</note> colored in red lead or black ink <note>original: "miniata vel atrata elementa." Red (minium) and black (atramentum) were the standard ink colors for titles and body text in manuscripts.</note>, but the great bodies of the world themselves—the works of His fingers. <note>A reference to Psalm 8:3: "When I consider thy heavens, the work of thy fingers, the moon and the stars, which thou hast ordained."</note> Thus He taught man to protect himself, to clothe himself, to raise his helpless offspring over a long period of time, to swim, and so forth. He did not do this as a vocal Master sitting beside him, nor by granting him weapons as He did for Bulls and Boars, or clothing as He did for sheep, or the ability to walk and swim from birth as He did for almost all cattle; instead, He gave him hands...

<summary>Kepler argues that God communicates through the physical universe rather than direct speech, using the planets as "letters" in a celestial alphabet. He explains that human survival and technology are learned through observing this natural order rather than through innate animal instincts.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Kepler’s exploration of celestial communication. Having established that God speaks through the "Book of Nature," Kepler now examines whether the "New Star" (the 1604 supernova) was intended specifically for humanity or for potential inhabitants of other worlds.</meta>

**ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT-BEARER. 195**

By what means will He reduce it to such constraints that He cannot act with us except through allegories and pictures? And this reasoning is challenged by the fact that we seem to both speak and hear in our sleep; doubtless we conceive the phantasms <note>original: "phantasias," referring to mental impressions or internal images</note> of speech and words no less meaningfully than other things. Furthermore, outside of man, among portents, there are also obscure <note>Greek original: "ἄδηλοι" (adeloi), meaning hidden, unclear, or non-manifest.</note> or absurd voices—as if an Ox, or an embryo, or Balaam’s donkey <note>A reference to Numbers 22:28, where God granted speech to a donkey to rebuke its master.</note> were to speak such things as can be understood. Therefore, so that he who defends these things may do so correctly, it is necessary to say either that there are many such natures—and rare ones at that—which express their thoughts through voices and the likenesses of voices, or indeed that all these modes belong to the same Creature <note>Kepler likely refers to the "Anima Mundi" or the rational spirit of the created universe.</note>, yet one is weaker and rarer than another.

Whichever of these opinions is true, this is established on both sides: that portents, omens, dreams, and things of this kind must be explained through allegories.

<margin>
Whether the
genuine meanings
of the star
overflow onto
the globe of
the Earth.
</margin>

Therefore, assuming that this star was lit by a certain plan, whether of God Himself or of a rational creature, if I am asked for what purpose I think this was done, and whether its meanings are to be drawn toward those things which we humans have at hand? First, I think not only individual nations, but even the entire globe of the Earth, is too small for all our thoughts arising from the genuine significance of a star dwelling in the highest ether to be poured into its embrace. For the vastness of the World is great; nor did that opinion of certain ancient Philosophers seem absurd to Tycho Brahe <note>Tycho Brahe (1546–1601) was Kepler’s mentor and the preeminent observational astronomer of the age.</note>,

<margin>
Whether there
are inhabitants
on the globes
of the stars.
</margin>

who maintain that the other globes, which are most vast, also have their own inhabitants—not humans, certainly, but other creatures. If these exist, they will certainly belong to the adornment of the world, and will not only perceive the effects of the stars, but will also be included in the providence of the supreme guardian. Nor will it be absurd for signs to be sent from that highest watchtower of the Sphere of fixed stars to them, just as to us humans living on the earth, perhaps more suited to their understanding than to ours. I ask, therefore, not for which nation, but rather for which globe entirely it should be believed that this star was lit.

While I am wandering in this way through the vastness of the world, I am called back again to this Earth of ours by the Great Conjunction of the three planets <note>The conjunction of Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars in 1604, which occurred in the same region of the sky where the New Star appeared.</note>, which stood around this star. For grant what is specific to this opinion: that this meeting of the star with that conjunction was not accidental, but sought by God; then you will certainly be convinced that it shone especially for the lands of Earth, and that this brilliance was assigned to the Earth and to no other globe besides. For nowhere except on this Earth was Jupiter seen joined to Mars on the day that the star shone forth. Let it be, then, that the star of the year 1572 <note>The supernova in Cassiopeia observed by Tycho Brahe thirty-two years earlier.</note> was lit perhaps for the Dog-heads <note>original: "Cynocephalis." Kepler refers to the satirical tales of Lucian of Samosata, who wrote of fictional alien races like the Dog-headed people.</note> of Lucian on Sirius, or...

<summary>Kepler discusses whether the 1604 supernova was a message for Earth or for inhabitants of other celestial bodies, concluding that its alignment with a planetary conjunction visible only from Earth proves it was intended for humanity.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Kepler’s argument that celestial phenomena are intended as messages for humanity. On the previous page, he suggested that portents and omens must be understood through allegories; here, he defends the significance of the Earth in the eyes of the Creator against Stoic philosophy.</meta>

196 **OF JOHANNES KEPLER**

...regarding the Minotaurs <note>original: "Minotauris"; likely referring to monstrous or irregular appearances, such as comets or anomalous sightings, seen in the constellation of Sagittarius</note> turning in Sagittarius; because they fell neither into the Ecliptic nor upon the joined planets. Certainly, this star of ours, because it stood near the Ecliptic, shone for the sake of the planetary spheres—whose motions it shows to us through the Zodiac—and indeed for the sake of this our Earth, from which Jupiter was seen to be conjoined with Mars and the new star. Seneca says somewhere <note>A reference to Seneca the Younger’s "Natural Questions," which often emphasized the insignificance of human affairs compared to the vastness of the cosmos</note> that nothing on these lands is so great that notice of it should reach that world above. In this entire book, I wage a most persistent war against this opinion. I shall answer Seneca from Chapter 16: to God the Creator, neither is that world above too vast, nor are these men too small. It is enough for us that we are sufficiently great in that we are the image of God the Creator.

<margin>
In what way the allegories of portents are to be interpreted.
</margin>

Therefore, hindered by nothing, I proceed to the probable Allegories which I, and others, have variously devised on the occasion of this star, and which various kinds of doctrine and philosophy produce for us. First, it seems to me: if celestial portents are set before us men by the counsel of God, it is consistent that those things which are plainly against the order of nature look toward the highest matters in human affairs. If, however, something is not against, but rather *beyond* the fixed state of nature, it looks toward some innovation in empires. Finally, if something is indeed according to nature, yet somewhat more unusual or rare, it pertains to the common people, to the things born of the earth, to animals, and other such things serving the utility of the common folk and their bodies.

Nothing on earth is greater than true religion and its perpetual cultivator, the Church. Therefore, God, the Best and Greatest, for the benefit of that people to whom He had given the first place in the Church, inhibited the course of the sun, changing the order of nature, and continued the space of the days—excluding the night—as a favor to Joshua alone, the leader of the Israelite people. At another time, when the pious King Hezekiah of that same people prayed for his safety, and after he had been encouraged by the words of the Prophet Isaiah, God did not hesitate to confirm it by the reversal of the solar shadow on the sundial <note>original: "Scroterico," a corruption of "sciotherico," referring to a shadow-measuring instrument or sundial</note>; a miracle astounding to all nations and contrary to the nature of things. This event gained such authority for Isaiah as was useful to the Church in all ages, for embracing with true faith those things which that Prophet had written most copiously in plainly divine words concerning the coming Messiah.

Now, when the Head of the Church, our King and Lord Jesus Christ, was atoning for the sins of the human race by a shameful death, no portent in heaven nor on earth, no horror could suffice, unless light itself—which is the most horrible of all horrible things <note>Kepler emphasizes that a midday darkness is the most terrifying disruption of the natural order</note>—was extinguished in a clear sky and at a time when it ought not to have been.

The author of the Epistle addressed to Dionysius the Areopagite to Polycarp <note>Kepler cites a letter attributed to Dionysius, though modern scholars consider these works "Pseudo-Dionysian"</note>—whoever he is, whether he tells a true history or imagines what might have been—attributes a speech to the philosopher Apollophanes which is not inconsistent with the matter he narrates.

<summary>Kepler rejects the Stoic view that human affairs are beneath divine notice, arguing that the New Star’s alignment with Earth proves its significance for humanity. He proposes a hierarchy for interpreting omens, where miracles represent religious shifts, while "extraordinary" natural events signify political changes.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Kepler’s exploration of celestial allegories. Having argued that God uses the heavens to communicate with humanity, Kepler now moves from general principles to specific historical and miraculous examples, such as the supernatural darkness recorded during the Crucifixion and the significance of comets versus fixed new stars.</meta>

ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT-BEARER. 197

<note>The "Serpent-bearer" refers to the constellation Ophiuchus, where the new star of 1604 appeared.</note>

Indeed, when both were watching this eclipse of the sun together in Heliopolis, Egypt, they noted that the Moon—contrary to the nature of its course—entered the disk of the sun from the east, and this at the time of the full moon. After it had stood still quietly under the sun for three hours, it turned its path and proceeded back toward the east, so that the beginning of the eclipse was from the eastern edge and the beginning of its emergence was from the western edge. Then Apollophanes added: "O good Dionysius, these are alternations of divine matters." <note>original Greek: "ὦ καλὲ Διονύσιε, θείων ἀμοιβαὶ πραγμάτων." This refers to a famous medieval legend involving Dionysius the Areopagite witnessing a miraculous eclipse during the Crucifixion of Christ.</note> He compared the ordinary courses of the planets to divine things because of the constancy of both; and he thought it impossible for the course of the moon to be turned into the contrary direction unless a change in divine affairs happened at the same time. Nor did he construct an unfitting allegory: the Moon signifies the human race; the Sun signifies the Son of God; the contrary course of the Moon, which it was obliged to take, signifies the sins of the human race; the eclipse of light signifies the passion of the Son of God. 

And let these be examples of the greatest things, expressed by portents of this kind, which are plainly contrary to the nature of the heavens. Now Comets and New stars—following this class of portents—are not indeed *against* nature, but yet are *beyond* the ordinary nature of the heaven. To what else should they be referred, which is nearest to the absolutely greatest human affairs, if not to empires, in which the safety of every individual man consists? Therefore, what a new Comet running across the sky is, such is some new "Comet-man" on earth: one who, whether by the greatness of his empire, or by arms, or by doctrine—whatever thing stirs up a particular fame for him—shakes a great part of the human race, just as a Comet does by terror, or as this man does by force brought upon them or some other calamity. 

In the year Alexander was born—who, by transferring the whirlwind of wars from Europe into Asia, brought the greatest slaughters upon the human race—Pliny records that there was a "Xiphias" <term>Xiphias: A sword-shaped comet, from the Greek word for sword</term> comet. In the year Mithridates was born, and in the year he took up his empire, horrible Comets shone.

If Comets running through the sky refer to individual Monarchs, then new stars <margin>Of what thing a new star is a sign.</margin> that do not run, but remain fixed in the same place, must refer to Republics or entire peoples, or to general causes pertaining to a great part of the human race.

He who enters upon this path will deduce that from this time, a new Republic is growing to maturity, by whose general empire the kingdoms that are today variously in turmoil shall one day be subdued: so that the world, which is now too restless and fierce, may rest for a while under the guardianship of this Monarchy. And if we add the conjoined planets, a double description will exist, according as we consider them. For if it is permitted and lawful to guess the mind of God from the simple nature of the Planets: because after three conjunctions—of Saturn and Jupiter, of Saturn and Mars, and of Jupiter and Mars (planets which are mobile and, when joined, are accustomed to disturb and move animal nature)—a single fixed star shone forth more lofty and bright, it seems to be signified...

Bb 3

<summary>Kepler interprets the 1604 new star as a political omen, distinguishing between moving comets (signifying individual rulers) and fixed new stars (signifying states or collective shifts). He suggests the star heralds a new era of stable governance or a "new Republic" that will eventually bring peace to a turbulent world.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Kepler’s discussion of the astronomical timing of the new star of 1604. He is exploring whether the star's appearance was a divine "seal" on the Great Conjunction of Jupiter, Saturn, and Mars, and how this aligns with both historical prophecy and the "Fiery Trigon" cycle.</meta>

198 JOHANNES KEPLER

...simply peace after the turmoils. What does this circumstance signify—that the star did not appear exactly at the location of the conjunction, but a little before it—certainly so that there would not be another conjunction of the **New Star** with the two planets. Thus, the moment of the last conjunction had already passed; for it was at noon on the 9th of October, when on the following evening Maestlin <note>Michael Maestlin was Kepler's former professor and a renowned astronomer who also observed the 1604 supernova.</note> had not yet seen the star blazing. Therefore, with this star as a guide:

*If any traces of our guilt remain,*
*They shall be made void and release the lands from perpetual fear.*
<note>original: "Si qua manent sceleris vestigia nostri / Irrita perpetua solvent formidine terras." From Virgil's *Fourth Eclogue*, a poem traditionally interpreted as prophesying a new golden age or the birth of a savior.</note>

With these verses, Virgil suggests that civil unrest must be removed and the very seeds of evil uprooted. For during the consulship of Pollio, to whom Virgil writes: *And this glory of the age shall begin with you, Pollio, as consul;* at the beginning of summer, [Octavian] Caesar and Antony, having made peace, entered Rome as friends. With what great zeal from the people and with what auspicious acclamations they were received can be seen from Dio <note>Cassius Dio, a Roman historian who recorded the transition from the Republic to the Empire.</note>; one of these acclamations is also this Virgilian address to Pollio. 

<margin>b</margin> <margin>Yet Saturn, left behind the star, was joined to it within two months; as if Virgil had also said this: *Yet a few traces of the ancient deceit shall remain.*</margin> <note>original: "Pauca tamen suberunt priscae vestigia fraudis." Kepler uses Virgil's poetry to allegorize the movement of the planet Saturn, traditionally associated with "deceit" or "melancholy."</note>

But if we accept this—that God had respect for astrology as it is commonly taught and practiced among men (a view supported by what I said in <margin>a</margin> <margin>Chapter XXV</margin>: that the star seems to have been ignited especially for astronomers, because it could not be so easily noticed by the common people as that earlier star of 1572 <note>The "New Star" of 1572 was Tycho Brahe's supernova in Cassiopeia; Kepler suggests the 1604 star required more expert eyes to distinguish from the nearby planets.</note>), then this star would be nothing other than a certain sealing of the Great Conjunction. It would be a confirmation of all those things which the most sober astrologers have said regarding the Great Conjunction according to the rules of the art, some of which we shall see below. Therefore, those things which we refuted from "naked Nature" in Chapter XXX would here be confirmed again by the arrival of the star, through this **very great postulate** <note>original Greek: "μέγα λίαν αἴτημα" (mega lian aitēma). Kepler uses this to mean a significant logical demand or assumption.</note>. For nothing is incredible anymore, once the **God from the machine** is summoned. <note>original Greek: "Θεὸς ἀπὸ μηχανῆς" (Theos apo mēchanēs). A theatrical term referring to a god appearing to solve a seemingly unsolvable plot point.</note> Therefore, both the time would be determined—a period of two hundred years—and the place: Hungary, Moravia, and Spain, which are assigned to the sign of Sagittarius. The Roman Empire, however, or the entire Christian Republic, would follow from this; just as these are the primary empires of the whole world, so Sagittarius is at this time the primary place in the heavens, as it is the beginning of the **Fiery Trigon** <note>The "Fiery Trigon" consists of the zodiac signs Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius. Astrologers believed Great Conjunctions occurring in these signs signaled major historical shifts every 800 years.</note> and the place where the three superior planets <note>Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn.</note> are conjoined.

Fabricius <note>David Fabricius, an East Frisian astronomer and frequent correspondent of Kepler.</note> adapts this specifically to Germany, with the argument that at the time the star could first be seen at sunset, the sign of Aries—the sign of Germany—and the equinox were rising. Thus, he draws the whole significance of the star toward a change in the Empire and gives it a name from the Eagle, the Imperial insignia. In this matter, he seems to me to be over-subtle; for various moments and various signs can be chosen in this way—such as Capricorn, because it was at the Midheaven when the sun was setting.
                                                                                                    Likewise...

<summary>Kepler discusses whether the 1604 star was a divine "confirmation" of the Great Conjunction's astrological significance, using Virgil's poetry to link celestial movements to historical peace and political shifts. He critiques the overly specific nationalistic interpretations of other astronomers like Fabricius.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Kepler’s analysis of the astrological and political significance of the New Star of 1604. He moves from pure astronomical observation to discussing the various interpretations held by his contemporaries at the Imperial court, specifically comparing the 1604 star to the supernova of 1572 and critiquing the prophetic timelines of Helisaeus Roeslin.</meta>

->ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT-BEARER. 199<-

likewise Pisces, because as the new star was culminating <note>reaching its highest point in the sky</note>, Pisces was rising; likewise Gemini, because as the new star was setting, Gemini rose. But much more so Sagittarius itself, not only because it provided a home to the three superior planets <note>Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn</note> and the new star, but also because, with the Sun positioned in the mid-heaven on the 10th day of October, the very location of the new star, 18 2/3° of Sagittarius, rises under a polar altitude of 48°. This is a point Fabricius <note>David Fabricius, a contemporary astronomer and correspondent of Kepler</note> would have emphasized with much better right as worthy of note. And what if we consider Libra, because as the star was rising, Libra was at its highest point and carried the Sun at its beginning? You see, therefore, that we remain with Sagittarius by a stronger right, lest the sign becomes uncertain. And because the star was simultaneously at the zenith for the Southern Ocean: someone might predict from all these combined factors a coupling of all those parts of the world under the rule of the Spaniards, or in general, of the Christians.

There were distinguished men in this court who, when they had read my comparison of this **New Star** with that of the year 1572 <note>Tycho Brahe’s supernova in Cassiopeia</note>—how that one withdrew to the side of the world into an obscure and lowly corner of the heavens, while this one has stood closest to the **royal path** <note>the ecliptic, the path the planets follow through the zodiac</note> of all the planets—they pondered the fact that in the year 1572 the Belgian provinces had newly withdrawn from their King. They dared to omen well for the Spaniard from this modern star: namely, that the Belgians, who had seceded from the King in 1572, would now return to the royal way, that is, to the rule of their King. For some time, favorable rumors nourished these hopes regarding peace between the Spanish and the Belgians, with the British mediating. For, deprived of the aid of neighboring Britain, and with those ports being open to the Spaniard in the future, they seemed ready to embrace safe counsels, distrusting their own strength.

The same reasoning also enticed Helisaeus Roeslin <note>A physician and astrologer who frequently published competing prophetic interpretations</note> to insist that the "Catastrophe of all European Affairs," which he had been harping on for so many years, was now imminent. No reasoning ever seemed so strange to me: that there was a Comet in the year 1556; then 24 years later, namely in the year 1580, another Comet changed its course during its duration and disappeared in Sagittarius, where after another 24 years, namely in the year 1604, a great conjunction of the Planets occurs: therefore, in the year 1604, there will be the **Catastrophe** <note>original: "Catastrophen," meaning a final resolution or major turning point</note> of all things begun since the year 1556.

A strange reasoning indeed, I say, for even before the year 1556 there were many Comets; and between the years 1556 and 1580 there were three or four others. Therefore, the connection of that shining one of 1556 with this one of 1580 is weak, deduced only from a contrary sign and contrary motion. But if that providence which exhibits the spectacle of Comets to men had intended for these two to be joined, why should I not rather say that the time of the **Catastrophe** of affairs conducted since the year 1556 began in the year 1580 and is clearly terminated in the year 1604? For the analogy would be better; those things signified by the direct Comet, which was in the year 1556, these things...

likewise Pisces, because as the new star was **culminating** <note>reaching its highest point in the sky relative to the observer</note>, Pisces was rising; likewise Gemini, because as the new star was setting, Gemini rose. But much more so Sagittarius itself, not only because it provided a home to the **three superior planets** <note>original: "tribus superioribus"; refers to Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn, the planets further from the Sun than Earth</note> and the new star, but also because, with the Sun positioned in the mid-heaven on the 10th day of October, the very location of the new star, 18 2/3° of Sagittarius, rises under a polar altitude of 48° <note>This latitude corresponds roughly to Southern Germany or Austria, where Kepler and his colleagues were observing</note>. This is a point Fabricius <note>David Fabricius, a Frisian astronomer and noted correspondent of Kepler</note> would have emphasized with much better right as worthy of note. And what if we consider Libra, because as the star was rising, Libra was at its highest point and carried the Sun at its beginning? You see, therefore, that we remain with Sagittarius by a stronger right, lest the sign becomes uncertain. And because the star was simultaneously at the **zenith** <note>the point directly overhead</note> for the Southern Ocean: someone might predict from all these combined factors a coupling of all those parts of the world under the rule of the Spaniards, or in general, of the Christians.

There were distinguished men in this court who, when they had read my comparison of this **New Star** with that of the year 1572 <note>The famous supernova in the constellation Cassiopeia observed by Tycho Brahe</note>—how that one withdrew to the side of the world into an obscure and lowly corner of the heavens, while this one has stood closest to the **royal path** <note>original: "via regia"; the ecliptic, or the apparent path of the Sun and planets across the sky</note> and in the very assembly of the planets—thought that some distinction of the same kind was being pointed out between the beginnings of the kingdoms of the North and the South. For they see that the star of 1572 appeared in the North, and shortly after, the kingdom of Navarre was increased by the addition of the kingdom of France; but this star of 1604, being Southern, they believe points toward the Southern kingdoms. <margin>A comparison of the new star with the one from the year 1572.</margin>

<meta>This page continues Kepler’s examination of the astrological prognostications surrounding the New Star of 1604, specifically engaging with the predictions made by Helisaeus Röslin regarding political upheavals in Europe.</meta>

**ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT-BEARER. 201**

Now the star shines, and the **Great Conjunction** <note>A rare astronomical event where Jupiter and Saturn appear close together in the sky; traditionally seen as a herald of major historical shifts</note> of the planets has occurred. And as I wrote, at this time a **catastrophe** <note>original: "Catastrophen"; here used in the classical sense of a "turning point" or "unraveling" of events, rather than just a disaster</note> of all things would occur, which in the Christian world had begun many years ago: this has already been partly fulfilled in England and now in the **Low Countries** <note>original: "Belgio"; referring to the ongoing conflict in the Netherlands</note>; the remaining parts will shortly appear, where they will turn out, namely the affairs of the King of the French, and the **Bishopric of Strasbourg** <note>original: "Episcopatus Argentinensis"</note>, and the Hungarian affairs.

He [Röslin] had written these things on **Thursday** <note>indicated by the symbol ♃ (Jupiter)</note>, October 4th in Alsace, when behold, the next day, **Friday** <note>indicated by the symbol ♀ (Venus)</note>, October 5th, the first and manifest beginnings of the Hungarian uprisings occurred: which for the Christians fighting against the Turk have so far been **thorns in the eyes** <note>original: "sudes sunt in oculis"; an idiom meaning a constant source of irritation or pain</note>. Meanwhile, the affairs of the Bishopric of Strasbourg have ended in a **turning point** <note>original: καταστροφὴν (katastrophēn)</note> milder indeed than Röslin had expected. And the King of the French **is at peace** <note>original: ἠρεμεῖ (ēremei)</note>: but nevertheless **he noted down both the moon and the doctrine and the thoughts of many** <note>original: κατέγραφε καὶ τὴν σελήνην καὶ τὸ δόγμα καὶ τὰς πολλῶν διανοίας (kategraphe kai tēn selēnēn kai to dogma kai tas pollōn dianoias)</note>, and what if they were Röslin's own? **And for some a catastrophe [downfall], for others who were ostracized, a restoration [return]** <note>original: Καὶ τῶν μὲν καταστροφὴ, τῶν δὲ ἐξοστρακισθέντων ἀναστροφή (Kai tōn men katastrophē, tōn de exostrakisthentōn anastrophē)</note>. Thus Röslin wrote almost nothing in vain in these words. Those things which he said regarding the conversion of the affairs of the Low Countries had then begun: they continued in the following year 1605 and this year 1606, with the Spaniards intercepting some fortifications from the eastern side. In England indeed, where a **downfall** <note>original: καταστροφὴ (katastrophē)</note> could then be hoped for or feared, as time followed, we have experienced a manifest **restoration** <note>original: ἀναστροφὴν (anastrophēn); specifically referring to the political stability following the Gunpowder Plot or the accession of James I</note>. So here nothing was according to his wish, except the movements of the Kingdom themselves in general, which for the most part tend up and down indiscriminately, like the waves of the sea.

But let us proceed to the remaining **conjectures and riddles** <note>original: εἰκασμοὺς καὶ αἰνίγματα (eikasmous kai ainigmata)</note>. For this guesswork of Röslin’s does not primarily follow the circumstances of the New Star itself, but assembles signs from many different times: which Tycho Brahe, in the first volume of his *Progymnasmata* on page 556, especially reproved in the work of **Gemma** <note>Cornelius Gemma, a physician and astronomer who wrote on the supernova of 1572</note>.

There were therefore those who, with an unlucky omen, noted that from being a very bright western star, it became a **livid** <note>pale or bluish-gray</note> eastern one: as if for either the Latin Church or the Western Empire, in that state in which it now flourishes, the most dismal condition of the eastern peoples were portended.

This circumstance could also be explained otherwise, namely that the things signified by the star would be greatest at the beginning, but afterwards, by the intervention of the **Magistrate** <note>In astrological political language, the "Magistrate" often refers to a secular ruler or high official</note>—who will be denoted by the Sun's approach to the star and signified by the Sun's splendor—the star would be hidden and snatched away from the minds and conversations of men, though not abolished; but after some delays had occurred, they would emerge again, though not in that vigor with which they began at first. Clearly, fit the Hungarian affairs here: with the hope of peace intervening, fear is deferred for a little while, until, as the peace negotiation vanishes, they break out again. But again, as in the beginning: if it is natural for a new star to gradually grow faint and finally die out, that natural condition—since it was not specially willed by God—cannot be ominous.

To some, the history of the movements, [written] by me in the script...

<meta>This page continues Kepler’s discussion of the interpretations of the New Star of 1604. He moves from discussing Helisaeus Röslin’s predictions to his own allegorical German writings and the popular tendency to speculate on celestial omens.</meta>

->202 **JOHANNES KEPLER**<-

In my German writing, I used riddling words <note>original: "ἀινίγματα" (ainigmata)</note> composed in this manner: At the assembly of **Saturn**, the stubborn old man, of proud **Jupiter**, and of combative **Mars**, celebrated in the house or temple of Jupiter: Jupiter and Mars, having formed a faction and joined their votes, elected a new star, which they would venerate with the same humility as they previously did Saturn. Once this was done, they each departed on their own course. Saturn, however, is slowly girding himself to set out toward the New Star; but it will happen that even he submits himself to the new one, provided that it lasts, and passes under it, though not much more humbly. This is indeed temporary; and the planets depart while the New Star remains: but they shall nonetheless return, at which time the New Star will undoubtedly be extinguished.

Nothing is said so lightly in this genre that the common people do not delight in trying to test it by guessing. I believe that just as physical rest is unpleasant to children, so do all people seek something in which to exercise themselves with conjectures; not so that they may find the truth, but so that they may cheat time by reasoning and speculating. Thus, this star first received me as its interpreter, and I found many other interpreters of my interpretation, among them **Fabricius** <note>David Fabricius (1564–1617), a German astronomer and theologian who corresponded with Kepler</note>. Nor were the Hungarian affairs poorly suited to this, if you understand the Imperial armies—going into Upper Hungary after the **Turk** was repulsed from **Strigonium** <note>The Latin name for the city of Esztergom in Hungary</note>—under the persona of Saturn. And yet, the motions of the heavens cannot be drawn into an omen with such subtlety. For they are necessary; they are not arbitrary, nor are they adapted by design. And I had warned in my German writing that I was placing a false premise as my foundation: that the star was ignited naturally from the **Conjunction of Jupiter and Mars** <note>Kepler later proved the star was a supernova, far beyond the planets, but here he critiques the astrological idea that the proximity of planets "caused" the star to appear</note>.

I had also emphasized this: At the time when astronomers were expecting the three most brilliant stars—Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars—to be joined in a single bond: suddenly a fourth, higher and brighter, intervened among those three; and the astronomers, letting go of those perennial ones, looked instead toward this transitory New Star. By this picture, it seems signified to us; not only through the way of nature, as said above in chapter 28, but also from the very will of the Signifier—whether **God** or a most powerful spirit: that something new is to be exhibited in human affairs, in which many people will be occupied, neglecting their private business.

Indeed, it is pleasing to predict even for the dead from their own philosophy. Well known is the dogma of **Pythagoras**, that the souls of **Heroes** ascend and descend through the **Milky Way**: And indeed the gate through which they begin to ascend lies open in **Capricorn**, while the one through which they descend is in **Cancer** <note>In ancient Neoplatonic and Pythagorean thought, these "Gates of the Sun" were the points where souls entered and exited the physical realm</note>. At this time, this opinion better supports itself, as the Milky Way extends from **Sagittarius** into Capricorn; whereas formerly it stretched through **Scorpio** and Sagittarius. Therefore, a star in the Milky Way in Sagittarius, and specifically a northern one, will signify that some Pythagorean Hero, having finished his period at this time, stands at the door of the lowest step, where...

He suggests the star represents a divine signal for a major shift in human affairs and references Pythagorean lore regarding the Milky Way as a path for souls.</summary>

where the ascent begins and the descent ends, it has come into this lower World even by making a leap. And a person of this sort will note that in the year 1572, a new star existed in the highest part of the Milky Way in Cassiopeia <note>This refers to the supernova observed by Tycho Brahe</note>; then in the year 1600, another appeared a little lower in the same Milky Way, in the constellation of the Swan <note>The star P Cygni, which brightened significantly in 1600</note>; and this one now is in the lowest place from the northern region of the Zodiac, closest to the Ecliptic. Therefore, it is not an ascent but a descent which has lasted 32 years.

From the ancient theology of the gentiles <note>original: "gentilium," referring here to pagan or classical mythology</note>, however, the blazing star, kindled in the rear foot of Aesculapius <note>Aesculapius is the Roman god of medicine, traditionally identified with the constellation Ophiuchus, the Serpent-bearer</note>—who grasps the serpent with his hands and treads upon the scorpion with his foot—will warn us that a most poisonous universal plague is imminent. It is as if this star were added to the foot of Aesculapius as a spur, so that he might put his foot forward, break off delays, and dispatch help; at the same time, remedies for this plague, such as scorpion oil <term>scorpion oil: a medicinal oil in which scorpions were infused, believed to cure stings and various infectious "venoms"</term> and theriac <term>theriac: a famous "universal antidote" from antiquity made of dozens of ingredients, including viper flesh, used against poisons and the plague</term>, would be recommended. And the origin of the plague from excessive moisture is divinely sign[ified]...

<meta>The text continues from page 203, finishing a sentence regarding the Magi seeking the newborn Christ, and then transitions into Kepler’s speculative interpretation of the New Star’s significance regarding global exploration, the conversion of the Americas, and the geopolitical conflicts between Christendom and the Ottoman Empire.</meta>

204 **JOHANNES KEPLER**

...to seek the King of the Jews, the Savior of the world, whose birth they believed was indicated by the Star. <note>Kepler is drawing a parallel between the Star of Bethlehem and the New Star of 1604.</note>

<margin>Whether the Indians <note>Refers to the indigenous peoples of the "New World" or Americas.</note> are to be universally converted.</margin>
It is a monumental thing that in this last century, a New World has been discovered by Christians, and within it have been found nations innumerable in multitude and so barbarous in their ferocity that you might almost believe some of the giants—who procured abortions and fed upon human flesh—had sprouted up in those regions from that universal flood.

Now we have the most certain prophecy of Christ our Savior: *This Gospel shall be preached in the whole world.* <note>Matthew 24:14</note> We have the example of the Canaanites, to whom God first gave four hundred years in the time of Abraham, and later forty years under Moses, for repentance; and when they made themselves worse, they were utterly rooted out. There is before our eyes a great abundance of men in Europe; we have implacable wars with the Turks <note>The Ottoman-Habsburg wars were a major contemporary conflict during Kepler's life.</note>; and domestic dissensions among ourselves. We are being pushed from the East to the West, where the Ocean receives us; we have at hand the arts and a stupendous skill in navigation, and a daily increasing zeal for reaching the new Indias. Nor are examples from antiquity lacking of how one nation expelled another—mostly the Scythians expelling the Europeans, and mostly the Orientals expelling the Westerners. What if, therefore, some massive *migration* <note>original: "ἀποικία" (apoikia), a Greek term for a colony or a "settlement away from home."</note> is imminent from all of Europe into those tracts of land: both the propagation of the Christian faith among the barbarians and their transition to civilization, as well as the extreme extirpation of those who do not obey, just as it was once for the Canaanites? Toward what does the finger of the star's circle point from the perspective of astronomy, or the sign of Sagittarius and the **Fiery Trigon** <term>Fiery Trigon: A set of three zodiac signs (Aries, Leo, Sagittarius) associated with the element of fire; the 1604 conjunction occurred within this cycle.</term> from astrology? Astrologers believe these favor the Christian religion, as does the **Spica** <term>Spica: The brightest star in the constellation Virgo; its position was traditionally linked to religious and agricultural cycles.</term> of the Virgin, with which, as Helisaeus Roeslin noted, the Sun stood at the rising of the star, illuminated in sextile <note>Sextile: An astrological aspect where two celestial bodies are 60 degrees apart, usually considered harmonious.</note> by the star. For we suppose that God uses these concepts of the astrologers to signify something to men.

<margin>Whether the sect of Mahomet <note>Muhammad</note> is signified by the star as being about to perish.</margin>
Prophecies of the Mahometans <note>Muslims</note> are circulated everywhere, from which many wish to prove that this is the time when their religion is to perish. These people will be pleased that God wished to indicate this very thing by the kindled New Star in Sagittarius, which is the triplicity <term>triplicity: A group of three zodiacal signs belonging to the same element.</term> of the Sun and Mars, and the house of Jupiter; since the Sun and Jupiter are said by astrologers (whose concepts we suppose God uses) to favor Christians, while Mars favors the Turks. And indeed, the star agreed more with Jupiter in its region of latitude, but Mars was at its maximum Southern latitude, which on this occasion could be seen as "depressed." From this, the victory of the Christian Religion over the Turkish one is astrologically concluded. But if you inspect the current worship of Christians, there were perhaps never better opportunities than today; let the Reader weigh the matter with himself. Is it impossible for God, out of these so manifold darknesses and contentions of Christ...

ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT-BEARER. 205

...of Christianity, to bring forth some light? Or is it incredible that among the causes for which heresies are permitted, this is also one: that the Turks, invited by these steps out of a sea of most shameful opinions, might strive to enter the ship of the Church? I know there are some steps by which several have defected from Christianity to Mohammedanism <note>original: *Mahumetismum*, referring to Islam.</note>. But I am pleased to argue with Saint Paul: *Have they stumbled so as to fall? Far from it! But through their transgression, salvation has come to the Mohammedan nations.* <note>Kepler is paraphrasing Romans 11:11, substituting "Gentiles" with "Mohammedan nations" to apply the scripture to his contemporary geopolitical situation.</note> Thus formerly the Goths, first converted to Christianity by the Arians <note>Arianism was an early Christian belief deemed heretical because it denied the full divinity of Christ; many Germanic tribes were converted to this version before joining the Roman Church.</note>, finally joined the Catholic Church entirely.

<margin>Whether the coming of the Messiah of the Jews is signified by the star.</margin>
It is well known that the Jews expect a Messiah who is to deliver them from the servitude of the nations. Saturn will be pleasing to them, as it was in agreement with the New Star in latitude, and was joined to it almost bodily on the 11th of December in the year 1604, and in the following August completed its station near it (see page 10), giving its name to the Great Conjunction <note>A Great Conjunction is the meeting of Jupiter and Saturn, which occurs every 20 years. Kepler believed the New Star appeared during a particularly significant "fiery" conjunction.</note>. Moreover, Saturn is assigned by astrologers to Judaism. And so, although we Christians acknowledge Jesus, the son of Mary from the tribe of David, who was born 1,600 years ago, as the true Messiah promised by the Prophets, and therefore worship Him most religiously as our Lord and God; and although we deplore the blindness of the Jews, who, having despised this Savior, long for another yet to come: nevertheless, many—and among them Tycho Brahe on folio 808—conclude that their expectation is not entirely in vain, based on the prophecy of Saint Paul to the Romans, Chapter 11, verse 25: *For I would not have you ignorant, brethren, of this mystery (lest you should be wise in your own conceits), that blindness in part has happened in Israel, until the fullness of the Gentiles \* has come in,* <margin>\* has entered <note>original: εἰσέλθῃ</note></margin> *And so all Israel \* would be saved. As it is written: THERE SHALL COME OUT OF ZION HE WHO \* SHALL SAVE AND DELIVER,* <margin>\* shall be saved <note>original: σωθήσεται</note></margin> *and shall turn away ungodliness from Jacob. And this is my covenant unto them, when I shall take away their sins.* Although I do not take upon myself the interpretation of such a difficult passage, I am nevertheless inclined, because of other circumstances, to believe that some general conversion of the Jewish people to the faith of Christ is imminent, which someone from among them, as an instrument of God, is to procure. For they are a most numerous people, who, though held in the highest contempt, nevertheless propagate themselves; who, through infinite persecutions, still find a place to dwell among the nations—and this is, without doubt, by divine disposition, for He directs the hearts of Kings as He wills. And what stands in the place of a supreme miracle is that they live off our labors while they themselves remain idle <note>Kepler reflects a common 17th-century prejudice, viewing Jewish involvement in trade and finance rather than manual labor as "idleness."</note>. Take away from the life of Christians their greed, inhumanity, neglect of neighbors, pride, pomp in dress, and lusts, and you would utterly root out the Jews, as they currently live, from the nature of things <note>Kepler suggests that the economic niche of the Jewish community is sustained only by the vices and needs of the Christian population.</note>. Therefore, the matter itself proclaims that even today this nation is part of divine providence. Thus it has happened that while all other nations have been confused and mixed together—so much so that very few families can trace their origin back a thousand years—the Jews alone remain, whose history is coeval with the world. Therefore, if
